3.360. A plan is only useful if it covers all relevant sites as required under the SEMD;
       South East Water agrees that schools with "sensitive customers" are vulnerable
        sites. 594 As such, we expect companies to proactively engage with these sites
        with greater needs during an emergency to establish what support is required
        and to reﬂect this within their emergency plans. An action from South East
        Water's 2018 Freeze Thaw Event Action Plan was to generate business continuity
        plans for schools. 595 In its 2019 Area Plans for the Relevant WRZs, South East
        Water noted the need for "comprehensive contingency plans and risk mitigation
        plans" to be implemented by March 2023 to enable recovery from operational
        issues, outage and incidents.596 In 2022, when South East Water reviewed the

        business continuity plans with schools were still "ongoing" (paragraph 3.327). We
        note in the 2023 High Demand Event, schools in the Eastern region were forced
        to close due to lack of guaranteed supply.


3.361. South East Water states it does not have the resources to develop business
       continuity plans for Tier 2 sites (which includes care homes), it relies on
        requests from retailers and that it does not need to have an emergency plan for
        Tier 3 sites. It does however need to have regard to the needs of non-domestic
        users as well as domestic users when developing emergency plans.597 Further,
        this does not detract from its SEMD duties in relation to vulnerable sites outlined
        above. The Five Acre Wood School example demonstrates that schools with
        vulnerable students need proactive engagement to establish what support is
        required and to reﬂect these within a company's emergency plan. We consider if
        South East Water had done this, this could have assisted identifying the issue of
        inadequate water supply at the Five Acre Wood School at an earlier stage.
        Further, the level of disruption experienced could have been reduced or avoided.

        we consider that such arrangements continue to not be in place. It has also

Summary of ﬁndings and conclusions on Licence Condition P12
3.366. As well as to ensure compliance with section 37 WIA91, incident preparedness is
       key to meeting South East Water obligations under the SEMD, which for the
       purpose of this investigation form part of South East Water's Regulated Activities
       under Licence Condition P12. [CONFIDENTIAL.] We have speciﬁcally examined
       the company's planning, systems and control and resources needed meet the
       following SEMD requirements:
     •     Ensure a minimum supply of water is available by alternative means during a
           civil emergency (paragraph 4(4)(b) SEMD).
     •     Plan to identify and prioritise vulnerable customers and vulnerable sites within
           its area (paragraph 4(4)(d) SEMD).
     •     Identify and assess any risks that may arise due to such dependencies to
           maintain its water supply during a civil emergency where it has arrangements
           with external suppliers of alternative water supplies (paragraph 4(7) SEMD).
     •     Regularly test the effectiveness of its incident plans to ensure they remain
           appropriate and to take steps to address any vulnerabilities identiﬁed
           (Paragraph 4(8) SEMD).

3.367. We found that, in contravention of Licence Condition P12, South East Water:
     •     Failed to have adequate systems of planning and internal control and
           management processes in place to monitor performance against supply
           interruptions to meet its PCL.
     •     Failed to make provisions for vulnerable sites within its emergency plan.
     •     Failed to implement actions that would have improved its incident response
           and plan to mitigate against known risks.

4. Our ﬁndings
4.1.   As required by sections 20 and 22A(4) of the WIA91, in this section we set out the
       statutory provisions and relevant licence conditions that we consider South East
       Water has breached and explain our assessment of the evidence we have
       considered in reaching our conclusions. We have also already set out our more
       detailed ﬁndings with respect to speciﬁc areas of South East Water's activities
       and performance in relation to its water supply system in Section 3.3.


4.2.   In the following sections, we consider each of the statutory provisions and
       relevant licence conditions that we consider South East Water has breached.

Section 37 WIA91: failure to maintain a water supply
system
4.3.   Maintaining water supplies to customers is a fundamental duty of water
       companies and vital to meeting the conditions of section 37 WIA91 and other
       duties. We have found several failures by South East Water to maintain an
       efﬁcient and economical system of water supply that in of themselves and
       collectively constitute a breach of section 37 WIA91. We made ﬁndings in section
       3.3 about South East Water's maintenance of key assets, its inadequate planning
       and its poor operational response and preparedness to the Events within the
       Relevant Period. This demonstrates a failure to develop and maintain an efﬁcient
       and economical system of water supply within its Eastern region and, separately,
       to ensure all such arrangements have been made for providing water supply to
       meet demand, as is necessary for securing its obligations under Part III. We
       have also found that South East Water has failed to ensure all such
       arrangements have been made for the provision of water supply and demand
       and for maintaining, improving and extending water mains, with respect to
       trunk mains repair and replacement, as are necessary for securing South East
       Water's obligations under Part III. We consider these are individual breaches of
       section 37 WIA91 but also when taken together indicate a widespread failure to
       maintain an economic and efﬁcient supply system and make arrangements
       required to meet its Part III obligations, in this case, its domestic supply duty
       and duties related to water quality.


4.4.   We recognise that other companies may have failed in certain respects in the
       past and during the Events within the Relevant Period. However, South East
       Water is the company that is consistently underperforming regarding supply
       interruptions and its handling of extreme weather. The number of ﬁndings
       identiﬁed in Section 3.3 demonstrate real issues with maintenance, planning

                                           168
       Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                on South East Water Limited



           and operational response and how South East Water maintains its supply
           system. Without further action from the company, we consider there is a real
           risk of supply interruptions in the future and that the company will continue to
           not meet its domestic supply duty. These issues have a real impact on
           customers, particularly those customers at "pinch points" at the "end-of-the-
           line" who disproportionately experienced supply interruptions compared with
           South East Water's Western region and other companies in the south east of
           England.


4.5.       Based on the evidence and ﬁndings set out in Section 3, we are satisﬁed that,
           during the Relevant Period, South East Water has contravened and is continuing
           contravene its duty under section 37 WIA91 by failing to develop and maintain an
           efﬁcient and economical system of water supply within its Eastern region and,
           separately, to ensure all such arrangements have been made for providing water
           supply to meet demand, as is necessary for securing its obligations under Part
           III, speciﬁcally:
       •     Failings in the inspection and maintenance of service reservoirs – South East
             Water did not ensure that service reservoirs were inspected, cleaned, and
             maintained at appropriate intervals, including the expected maximum ten-
             year inspection cycle. As a result, asset condition deteriorated over time,
             increasing the risks to both water quality and continuity of supply.
       •     Failings in the management of storage levels in advance of and during
             critical periods– South East Water did not adequately manage storage to
             maintain sufficient headroom at service reservoirs. We identified instances
             where key reservoirs entered high-risk periods with critically low storage
             levels, heightening the likelihood and severity of supply interruptions.
       •     Failings in the inspection, maintenance, and optimisation of boreholes –
             Inadequate inspection and maintenance resulted in boreholes becoming non-
             operational or underperforming, increasing the risk of outages. On several
             occasions, boreholes were unavailable during periods of high demand while
             other assets were also out of service, reducing overall system yield. In
             addition, South East Water did not take timely steps to optimise the output of
             non-operational boreholes or to reprioritise its borehole maintenance
             programme to improve supply resilience.
       •     Failings in maintenance planning and risk assessment of key assets – South
             East Water did not adequately plan maintenance activities outside of critical or
             high demand periods and lacked a holistic assessment of system-wide risks.
             In particular, it did not sufficiently assess the cumulative impact of asset
             outages on available supply or ensure that adequate operational headroom
             and resources were in place to avoid or mitigate loss of supply events. Nor did
             it mitigate known risks posed by extreme weather and climate change,


                                              169
Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                         on South East Water Limited



     including implementing the actions and recommendations from the DWI's and
     Ofwat's review of the 2018 Freeze Thaw Event.
•    Failings in planning for its water resources within its water supply system at
     a sub-zonal level – South East Water failed to manage its supply demand
     balance and headroom at a sub-zonal level which resulted in the company
     being with less headroom and less able to respond than we would expect a
     reasonable company to have be able to in the lead up to, and during the
     Events in the Relevant Period. Sub-zonal planning is particularly important for
     South East Water's Eastern region because some sub-zonal areas lack
     interconnectivity and have single points of failure.
•    Failings in its operational response to supply interruptions – South East Water
     failed to maintain a sufficient operational response to supply interruption
     incidents leaving customers at risk of being unduly without water during peak
     periods. In particular:
       • Failure to respond promptly at the start of incidents, using all available
          operational tools.
       • Failure to deploy alternative water supplies effectively, including bottled
          water, tankers, and mutual aid arrangements.
       • Failure to plan for and prioritise vulnerable sites within its emergency
          plan to ensure support reached those most at risk.

    Failure to develop and maintain an efﬁcient and economical system of water
    supply within its Eastern region and, separately, to ensure all such
    arrangements have been made for maintaining, improving and extending its
    water mains and other pipes, as is necessary for securing its obligations under
    Part III, speciﬁcally:

       •   Failings in the preventative maintenance of trunk mains. South East
           Water relied disproportionately on reactive maintenance and did not
           undertake sufficient preventative maintenance or renewal of its trunk
           mains. These assets were a contributing factor to supply interruptions in
           the Eastern region.




                                       170
      Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                               on South East Water Limited



Licence Condition P12: South East Water's systems of
planning and internal control
4.5       Licence Condition P12 requires a company to act in a manner best calculated to
          ensure that it has in place sufﬁcient ﬁnancial and management resources and
          systems of planning and internal control to carry out its regulated activities,
          both now and for the foreseeable future.


4.6       South East Water should have understood its legal and regulatory obligations,
          including those relating to section 37 WIA91 outlined above, and have put in
          place appropriate processes and controls to ensure that it was able to operate
          its water supply system assets in a manner that complied with those obligations.
          It should have in place systems to ensure that any non-compliance (and the risk
          of non-compliance) is escalated within the company and that prompt, timely
          investigations are carried out and remedial action taken to secure compliance.
          It should also have internal controls in place that follow-up these interventions
          to ensure that compliance is achieved in a timely and effective manner.


4.7       We found that South East Water was, at best, reactive with respect to
          maintenance, which is not the approach of a reasonable company planning to
          meet its regulated activities. The evidence outlined in Section 3 shows an
          overarching failure of South East Water to maintain its supply system at a
          sufﬁcient production capacity to meet periods of peak demands and that its
          asset base was operating at a level that did not support proper functioning of its
          water supply system. South East Water's Executive Team and Board were aware
          of these issues and should have been doing more to improve its compliance,
          including with respect to its operational response to Events in the Relevant
          Period, with its regulated activities in section 37 WIA91 and the SEMD.

4.8       Based on the evidence and ﬁndings set out in Section 3, we are satisﬁed that,
          during the Relevant Period, South East Water has breached and continues to be
          in breach of Licence Condition P12 by failing to ensure it had adequate ﬁnancial
          and management resources and systems of planning and internal control,
          speciﬁcally:
      •     Failures to establish and implement effective programmes for the routine
            inspection and maintenance of key assets. In particular:
              • Failure to carry out regular inspections and maintenance of service
                 reservoirs – Despite Board level awareness of the condition related risks
                 associated with service reservoirs, South East Water did not ensure that
                 effective systems were in place to secure timely inspection and


                                              171
Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                         on South East Water Limited



         maintenance. It also did not have a clear and credible plan to remediate
         assets that were known to be deteriorating.
      • Failure to put in place a structured planned preventative maintenance
         regime for trunk mains (including strategic valves) – South East Water
         lacked a comprehensive and systematic approach to preventative
         maintenance for these assets, including mechanisms to reliably monitor
         and track the delivery of maintenance activities. This limited its ability to
         manage asset condition proactively and reduce the likelihood of failures.
      • Failure to proactively maintain and optimise borehole yield – South East
         Water made a deliberate decision not to invest in borehole maintenance
         and replacement over an extended period of approximately ten years,
         despite recognising that such investment would enhance supply
         resilience. Although the company has cited budgetary constraints, our
         ﬁndings demonstrate that boreholes play a critical role in providing
         additional resilience in the Eastern region, where the supply system is
         known to be vulnerable.
•   Failures to plan maintenance activities in a way that had sufﬁcient regard for
    peak periods and system-wide risk – South East Water did not sufﬁciently
    schedule maintenance outside of critical or high demand periods, nor did it
    adequately assess the impact that asset outages would have on overall supply.
    In particular, it failed to take proper account of other assets that were already
    out of service and did not ensure that sufﬁcient operational resources and
    headroom were available to prevent or mitigate supply interruption incidents.
•   Failures in storage planning at service reservoirs ahead of critical or high
    demand periods – South East Water did not have robust planning
    arrangements to ensure that storage levels in service reservoirs were
    maximised ahead of high-risk periods, such as summer and winter. This
    included a failure to appropriately consider minimum storage requirements
    necessary for the effective operation of service reservoirs and booster
    pumping stations, increasing the risk of supply shortfalls during periods of
    elevated demand.
•   Failing to use the planning tools available to it such as its area plans, PWPC,
    up-to-date hydraulic models, root cause analysis and to mitigate known risks
    posed by extreme weather and climate change, including implementing the
    actions and recommendations from the DWI's and Ofwat's review of the 2018
    Freeze Thaw Event.
•   Failings in using internal targets and plans to manage key assets – South
    East Water did not implement effective internal controls to review or update
    the actions and principles set out in its Area Plans introduced in 2019 and
    2020, nor did it fully comply with relevant DWI notices regarding service
    reservoirs.


                                      172
       Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                on South East Water Limited



       •     Failings in preparedness and resilience for supply interruptions – South East
             Water failed to adequately plan for and manage supply interruptions, including
             establishing what support is required for vulnerable sites and reﬂecting this in
             its emergency plan, mitigating known risks, ensuring operational staff had the
             right information and controls, maintaining alternative water supply
             arrangements, and deploying sufﬁcient resources to prevent customers being
             unduly without water during peak periods.
       •     Failings in Executive and Board level oversight and assurance – South East
             Water’s Executive Team and Board did not ensure that adequate reporting
             metrics and management information were in place to provide a clear and
             accurate understanding of the company’s compliance with its general duty
             under section 37 WIA91. In addition, it failed to effectively monitor and
             challenge risks to supply system resilience, to properly scrutinise compliance
             with legal and regulatory obligations, and ensure that timely and appropriate
             action was taken in response to those risks.


Licence Condition P: other requirements
4.9        Our investigation has also considered other aspects in the ownership and
           management of South East Water's water supply system – both at an operational
           and senior management and board levels relevant to other requirements in
           Licence Condition P. While we make no ﬁndings on these points, we set out our
           observations on Licence Condition P3 and P30 below. Licence Condition P30
           imposes certain assurance and reporting requirements on companies' boards,
           including a ring-fencing certiﬁcate (paragraph 2.72). Licence Condition P3
           speciﬁes the Board Leadership, Transparency and Governance Objectives.
           Below, we consider these additional issues related to South East Water's failure
           to have adequate processes in place to ensure oversight of compliance,
           including at Board level.


4.10       For its RFCs and RCSs throughout the Relevant Period, 602 South East Water's
           Board conﬁrmed that the company had:
       •     a full understanding of, and was meeting, all its relevant statutory, licence and
             regulatory obligations and had taken steps to understand and meet customer
             expectations;

             Section 3.3 which did not ﬁnd the mitigating actions in its Action Plan to be
             sufﬁcient to address the contraventions of section 37 WIA91 and Licence

             Period.

             management should have had plans to keep those Area Plans up to date and to
             monitoring the delivery of those mitigating actions.


4.13         Further, the Executive and Board should have ensured that South East Water
             followed up and actioned lessons learnt from past Events and taken steps to
             identify the root cause(s) and address them. Despite high level engagement at
             CEO/Chair level between South East Water and Ofwat in July 2023, it was not
             until 1 May 2024, after we requested this on the opening of this investigation on
             15 November 2023, that South East Water provided us with the Action Plan.
             While South East Water maintain that it would have reprioritised schemes
             without Ofwat intervention, the content of its Action Plan remained lacking in
             terms of addressing the root cause of supply resilience issues (as outlined in
             Section 3.3.2) and remained focussed on investment solutions rather than on
             maintaining key assets and improving its systems of planning. Our ﬁndings show
             that planned preventative maintenance was necessary to address the known
             vulnerabilities within South East Water's Eastern region. As such, we consider
             there was sufﬁcient awareness of the supply system resilience issues at the
             Executive and Board level, however, a lack of ownership and accountability to

put in place appropriate mitigating actions that focussed on root cause of these
issues. As such we are not satisﬁed that South East Water's Executive and Board

ensured that the company took appropriate action in response to those risks.
This is counter to the requirement in Licence Condition P3.3 which requires that
the board's leadership and approach to governance ensures trust in the
company and accountability for its actions.

5. Proposal to issue an enforcement order
Background
5.1.   Under section 18 WIA91, if we are satisﬁed that a regulated company is
       contravening or likely to contravene a condition of its Licence or a statutory
       requirement we are under the duty to make an enforcement order containing
       such provisions as are requisite to secure compliance with that condition or
       requirement.


5.2.   Under section 19 WIA91, we are not required to make an enforcement order if we
       are satisﬁed that the contravention in question is of a trivial nature, or is
       contrary to our duties in Part I WIA91, including our duty to have regard to best
       regulatory practice and our other duties under section 2 WIA91, explained in
       more detail in Section 2 above. We may also instead or in part accept formal
       undertakings from a company to take remedial steps as long as we consider
       them appropriate to secure compliance. A company's obligation to comply with
       an undertaking is treated as a statutory requirement which is enforceable by
       Ofwat under section 18 WIA91. As also explained above, in carrying out our
       enforcement duty under section 18 WIA91, we must also have regard to the
       desirability of promoting economic growth.


5.3.   In pursuance of the duties described above, and having regard to the growth
       duty guidance, we have therefore sought to ensure these proposed enforcement
       order measures are proportionate to the extent of non-compliance we have
       found and a company returns to compliance as quickly and efﬁciently as
       reasonably possible, consistent with what the company can reasonably deliver.


5.4.   Under section 22 WIA91, where a company fails to comply with an enforcement
       order, Ofwat may apply to the Court to seek an injunction to compel a company
       to do so or for other forms of appropriate relief. Section 22 WIA91 also makes
       clear that the obligation to comply with an enforcement order constitutes a duty
       owed to any person who may be affected by a contravention of the order. That
       person may bring legal proceedings against a company if a breach of that duty
       causes them to sustain loss or damage.


5.5.   Further information regarding considerations relevant to enforcement orders
       can be found in our published guidance (Enforcement Guidance here by
       hyperlink).




                                           176
       Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                on South East Water Limited



Competition Act 1998
5.6.   Under sections 19(1A) and 19(2A) WIA91, before deciding to make an
       enforcement order or conﬁrm a provisional enforcement order, Ofwat is required
       to consider whether it would be more appropriate for us to proceed under the
       CA98. Ofwat is satisﬁed that it is more appropriate to make an order pursuant to
       section 18 WIA91 in this case because there are speciﬁc statutory and Licence
       provisions closely related to the issues identiﬁed in this investigation which we
       can enforce under that section.

The enforcement order in this case
5.7.   As explained in Section 4, we have found that South East Water had acted in
       breach of its duties under section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12.
       Accordingly, at Appendix 0 we enclose a draft enforcement order (the Order)
       which sets out the terms we consider necessary to ensure compliance with
       these provisions. We are satisﬁed that the provisions of the Order are requisite
       for the purpose of ensuring compliance with section 37 WIA91 and Licence
       Condition P12. We consider that its requirements, including the requirement for
       the company to develop a remediation plan, are aimed at protecting customers
       and ensuring that South East Water properly carries out its functions as well as
       securing long-term resilience of its supply system through better planning,
       maintenance and operational response. They do so proportionately, in particular
       within the context of a wide range of failings we have identiﬁed in this
       investigation, as they are subject to ﬁnanceability, affordability and
       deliverability considerations, which will inform the discussions between the
       company and Ofwat when agreeing the remediation plan.


5.8.   The draft enforcement order requires South East Water to conduct an
       independent third-party review of its root cause analysis and maintenance
       programmes to ensure planned preventative maintenance is regularly
       undertaken and prepare an appropriate plan to address the issues identiﬁed
       taking account of deliverability, affordability and ﬁnanceability. In setting this
       requirement, we have considered the short term actions identiﬁed in South East
       Water's Action Plan and the longer term actions, including investment and
       prioritisation. Without thorough root cause analysis we are not satisﬁed that
       these actions alone are sufﬁcient to bring South East Water into compliance. We
       recognise that it may require a considerable period (i.e. over multiple AMPs) for
       South East Water to resolve the issues with respect to its compliance with
       section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12. However, as outlined in Sections 3
       and 4 our main ﬁndings are not in relation infrastructure investment but have
       focussed on South East Water's maintenance of key assets, planning and

                                            177
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



         operational response. As such, we consider it necessary for South East Water to
         provide us with a plan for preventative maintenance programmes that are
         needed to address the known vulnerabilities within its Eastern region until such
         time as longer term investment (if appropriately supported by root cause
         analysis and optioneering) can take place to improve interconnectivity. As we
         advised it on 16 May 2025, South East Water will need to address the entirety of
         the concerns raised within this investigation to demonstrate appropriate root
         cause analysis has been undertaken and that it has updated its hydraulic
         models to ensure robustness in its process.

5.9.     As above, we have also considered ﬁnanceability and whether it would be
         economical to meet the requirements of the Order. As outlined in Section 3.3.2,
         South East Water has been provided with base expenditure. It has demonstrated
         it is able to reprioritise funding in response to this investigation. It has also
         overspent in previous periods where it considered that funding was needed to
         meet its duties. The PR24 Final Determination gave South East Water a
         £50 million contingency allowance for resilience expenditure.604 As such we
         expect it undertake the necessary expenditure to meet its obligations under
         section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12.

Effects of Proposed Order
5.10.    Under the Proposed Order, South East Water will be required to:


a) Obtain a report from an independent third party reviewer (approved by Ofwat)
    examining its approach to:
(i) Review its root cause analysis process and approach to identify the underlying
     causes of failings to comply with section 37 WIA91 including maintenance, planning
     and operational response;
(ii) Review its incident management procedures, including operational tools and
      escalation triggers; deployment of alternative water supplies and implementing
      lessons learnt from previous incidents.
(iii)  Review the scope of the company's root-and-branch review of hydraulic models;
(iv) Conduct a root cause analysis of the failings identiﬁed in this decision;
(v) Make recommendations for remediating the failings, taking into account
    deliverability, affordability and ﬁnanceability.
b) Prepare a remediation plan (for approval by Ofwat), applying the recommendations
   from the independent reviews, setting out the further actions it needs to undertake



604
   See Table 3.3 - PR24-redeterminations-response-to-South-East-Waters-statement-of-case-REDACTED-
redacted.pdf p.59.

                                               178
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



   to demonstrate how it will invest in and maintain resilience within its water supply
   system to minimise the risk of supply interruptions to its customers.
c) In line with our ﬁndings in this draft decision, we also require South East Water to
   undertake the following actions:
(i) Conduct a root-and-branch review of all its hydraulic models.
(ii) Establish what support is required for vulnerable sites and including this within its
      emergency plan.
(iii)   Conduct a thorough lessons learnt, including root cause analysis, one month
   after each water supply interruption incident in the future.
d) Prepare (and report to Ofwat) on a management plan to ensure its senior
   management and Board actively review and challenge compliance with its general
   duty under section 37 WIA91 and the SEMD.


5.11.       By virtue of section 22 WIA91, the Order would also have the following effects:
        •     The obligation to comply with an enforcement order shall be a duty owed to
              any person who may be affected by a contravention of the Order.
        •     In any proceedings brought against any company in the pursuance of
              paragraph 5.4, other than proceedings in respect of so much of a
              contravention of any order as consists in a breach of a duty imposed by virtue
              of section 68(1)(a) or (1A)(a) WIA91, it shall be a defence for the person to
              show that the person took all reasonable steps and exercised all due diligence
              to avoid contravening the order.
        •     Without prejudice to any right which any person may have by virtue of
              paragraph 5.4 to bring civil proceedings in respect of any contravention or
              apprehended contravention of an enforcement order, compliance with any
              such order shall be enforceable by civil proceedings by the relevant
              enforcement authority for an injunction or any other appropriate relief.

                                on South East Water Limited




6. Proposal to impose a ﬁnancial penalty and
the proposed level of that penalty
Relevant provisions of the WIA91
6.1.    Under section 22(1)(a) and 22A(2)(a) of the WIA91, Ofwat may impose on the
        water or sewerage company a penalty of such amount (not exceeding 10% of the
        regulated company's relevant turnover in a relevant year) as is reasonable in all
        the circumstances of the case, where it is satisﬁed that the company has
        contravened or is contravening any condition of its Licence, or any statutory
        requirement Ofwat is responsible for enforcing.


6.2.    Under section 22A(11) of the WIA91, no penalty imposed by Ofwat under section
        22A may exceed 10% of the turnover of the company, determined in accordance
        with the Water Industry (Determination of Turnover for Penalties) Order 2005.
        Article 3 of that Order provides for the purposes of section 22A(11) of the WIA91,
        the relevant turnover of a company is the applicable turnover for the preceding
        business year net of tax (that is, the last business year preceding the date on
        which Ofwat gives notice under section 22A(4) of the WIA91). Article 2(2) deﬁnes
        "applicable turnover" as "the amounts, determined in conformity with normal
        accounting practice in the United Kingdom, which are derived by a company
        from the provision of goods and services, in the course of the company's
        regulated activities in respect of which the penalty is imposed" and Article 2(1)
        deﬁnes "regulated activities" as: "(a) in the case of a company holding an
        appointment as a water undertaker, its functions as a water undertaker." In this
        case, the regulated activities in question are related to South East Water's
        activities as a water company. As South East Water is a water only company, this
        captures all of its activities, represented by its total appointed revenue.


6.3.    South East Water's last business year ran from 1 April 2024 to 31 March 2025. Its
        applicable turnover derived from its activities as a water company in that
        business year was £280.811 million. 605 Accordingly, the maximum penalty that
        we could impose on the company would be 10% of that number i.e.
        £28.08 million.




  Total appointed revenue from Table 2I: South East Water Regulatory Accounts for year ended 31 March
605

2025, p.71.

                                                 180
       Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                on South East Water Limited



6.4.   Under section 22C(1) WIA91, Ofwat may only impose a penalty for breaches
       which occurred within ﬁve years of the penalty being imposed, unless, before
       the end of that period, a notice under section 203 WIA91 is issued or a
       provisional or ﬁnal enforcement order is issued. In this case, as we issued a s203
       notice on 15 November 2023, this means that we can apply a penalty in respect
       of breaches that occurred after 15 November 2018.


6.5.   Ofwat is satisﬁed that the breaches for which a penalty is being proposed fall
       within the time period for imposing a penalty under section 22C(1) WIA91.

6.6.   Under section 22A(4) WIA91, we must specify the acts or omissions and any
       “other facts” which we consider justify imposing a penalty for a relevant
       contravention, and of the amount proposed.

6.7.   Pursuant to section 22B(2) WIA91, Ofwat must have regard to its most recently
       published statement of policy with respect to ﬁnancial penalties (Statement of
       Policy) when deciding whether to impose a penalty, and its amount. The growth
       duty in section 108 of the Deregulation Act 2015 (and subsequent sections) also
       applies to the imposition of a penalty. The factors set out in our Statement of
       Policy have guided us in considering whether a penalty is necessary and
       proportionate consistent with the growth duty. For the reasons we provide below
       in our consideration of whether to propose a penalty and, if so, how much, we
       consider that a penalty is necessary, and the amount we are proposing is
       proportionate .


6.8.   Section 22A(9) WIA91 provides that penalties imposed under section 22A WIA91
       are paid into HM Treasury's Consolidated Fund.

Competition Act 1998
6.9.   Sections 22A(13) and 22A(14) WIA91 provide that imposing a ﬁnancial penalty
       Ofwat must consider whether it would be more appropriate to proceed under the
       CA98. As stated in Section 5, Ofwat is satisﬁed that it is more appropriate to
       proceed under section 22A WIA91 given that it enables Ofwat to impose
       penalties in respect of speciﬁc statutory and Licence provisions closely related
       to the issues identiﬁed in this investigation.




                                           181
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



Reasons for proposal to impose a penalty and the proposed broad
level of that penalty, including acts, omissions and other relevant
facts
6.10. As set out in Section 3 and Section 4, we are satisﬁed that South East Water has
      contravened section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12 in relation to how it
            managed, operated and maintained its water supply system during the Relevant
            Period. We note that in this document we have considered the broader context
            for South East Water's conduct preceding the Relevant Period to understand
            whether the company should have been aware of the risks to its water supply
            system and/or learned lessons from the issues it had experienced, including its
            performance during the 2018 Freeze Thaw Event. However, we have not applied
            a penalty with respect to the company's conduct prior to the Relevant Period.


6.11.       In determining whether to impose penalties for these contraventions, we have
            had regard to Ofwat’s Statement of policy with respect to ﬁnancial penalties and
            in particular the following factors, as further detailed below:
        •     The seriousness and duration of the contraventions or failure.
        •     If the contravention or failure has damaged the interests of customers, the
              degree of harm caused, and any increased costs incurred by customers.
        •     If the application of a penalty would be likely to create an incentive to comply
              and deter future contraventions or failures.
        •     Any gains made by the company (financial or otherwise).
        •     Any damage to other market participants.
        •     Any damage caused to the environment.
        •     Whether the contraventions or failure was or is of a trivial nature.
        •     Whether the contravention or possibility of a contravention would have been
              apparent to a diligent company.
        •     Precedents set under equivalent provisions for other utilities.

6.12.       In considering these factors we rely on the evidence gathered in each speciﬁc
            case.

Seriousness and duration of the contravention

6.13.       As a general duty, section 37 WIA91 is one of the core obligations of a water
            company. Licence Condition P12 similarly relates to fundamental requirements
            for a company to act in the manner best calculated to be able to fulﬁl its
            functions and duties. We therefore consider a contravention of these provisions
            to be serious.




                                                182
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



6.14.   The evidence of our ﬁndings in Sections 3 and 4 shows that South East Water
        has failed to maintain, manage and operate key assets within its asset base in
        the manner we would expect from a reasonable company, with respect to both
        section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12, and that it is currently still
        contravening those provisions. As set out in detail in Sections 3 and 4, these
        failings are widespread across different asset types and concern the key aspects
        of running a water supply system, namely: failure to timely, effectively and
        proactively inspect and maintain a number of key assets, in line with the basic
        expectations we have of a reasonable company, failure to effectively plan for
        critical demand periods, and failures in operational response to address the
        underlying issues and assist the customers affected by the supply interruptions
        and emergencies. They have persisted over an extended period of time (at least
        ﬁve years going back to 2018) in all WRZs in the Eastern region, resulting in
        South East Water failing to deliver the continuity of supply expected by
        customers and putting water quality and future supply at a signiﬁcant risk. They
        stem from its failure to address known vulnerabilities in the Eastern region that
        were known to the company from at least the start of the Relevant Period.
        Throughout our ﬁndings, we have highlighted the impact on customers of these
        failures, including those repeatedly affected by Events during the Relevant
        Period particularly customers at the "end-of-the-line" (we discuss this further
        below). In addition, our ﬁndings in Sections 3 and 4 demonstrate that South
        East Water has not historically had an adequate understanding of how its assets
        have been operating in practice, and, to date, has failed to undertake basic
        adequate root cause analysis that we would expect of a reasonable company to
        get to the underlying cause of issues with its supply system. Instead, South East
        Water relies on its view that the root cause of Events during the Relevant Period
        was "extreme weather" or "high demand" (rather than these being triggering
        factors), and did not seek to ﬁnd out internal asset heath, process and planning
        related causes that led to repeat instances of non-compliance. As set out in
        Sections 3 and 4, these breaches are ongoing.

If the contravention or failure has damaged the interests of customers, the degree of
harm caused, and any increased costs incurred by customers.

6.15.   As set out in our ﬁndings in Sections 3 and 4, the evidence obtained during our
        investigation indicates that South East Water's customers in its Eastern region
        have not received the level of service that they are entitled to from a statutory
        provider of water services. The ongoing nature of the breaches that we have
        identiﬁed mean that we are not conﬁdent that there will not be further harm to
        customers in the future.




                                            183
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



6.16.   Section 3 outlines the customer impact, including areas that have been
        impacted by more than one of the Events during the Relevant Period (see
        Appendix 10). These events affected a total of 286,645 customers. Ofwat and
        CCW research provided a qualitative assessment of the impact of supply
        interruptions in relation to 43 customers affected by the 2023 High Demand
        Event, which included loss of trust in South East Water and that its customers
        felt "blamed" during that event.


6.17.   Our investigation has speciﬁcally highlighted customers in areas at the "end-of-
        the-line" who are supported by booster pumps and vulnerable to supply
        interruptions, for example, this has affected customers in Loose, Charing Hill,
        Balcombe, Butlers Green and Cuckﬁeld. Speciﬁcally, we have identiﬁed Five
        Acre Wood School a vulnerable site in the Loose area, which needed additional
        support in South East Water's emergency plan and additional measures to
        improve resilience. While South East Water has taken steps to provide a remedy
        for this speciﬁc site, it is not clear how this situation would have been remedied
        or captured without the direct engagement of Ofwat and MPs.


If the application of a penalty would be likely to create an incentive to comply and
deter future contraventions or failures

6.18.   We consider that a ﬁnancial penalty issued under section 22A WIA91 will act as
        an incentive to deter future contraventions or failures. From even before the
        Relevant Period, South East Water had opportunities to improve its performance,
        in particular the lessons it ought to have learned from previous supply
        interruption events prior to and during the Relevant Period (such as the 2018
        Freeze Thaw Event following the Beast from the East) and subsequent reviews in
        2018 and 2022, which resulted in Ofwat and the DWI publishing a set of
        recommendations to companies). As above, we have found South East Water's
        contraventions to be ongoing and we consider that a penalty, alongside the
        propose enforcement order, will serve as an incentive for the company to return
        to compliance as soon as practicably and reasonably possible.


6.19.   We have not been satisﬁed with the level of ownership from the company and
        willingness to address the supply resilience issues in response to our
        investigation to affect real change for its customers and consider that a
        signiﬁcant penalty is necessary to change the company culture/behaviour
        creating an incentive to comply and to deter future contraventions.




                                            184
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



Any gains made by the company from its contraventions

6.20. We are not aware of any gains made by South East Water from its
      contraventions. Therefore, we have not found evidence that the company made
        ﬁnancial gains from the contraventions identiﬁed.

Any damage to other market participants

6.21.   South East Water's contraventions have contributed to a loss of trust and
        conﬁdence in the water sector among customers and wider stakeholders. There
        has been signiﬁcant reporting on its supply interruptions, which has
        contributed to a negative impression of the whole sector.

6.22.   The impact of this is to reduce conﬁdence in the regulatory regime and to
        potentially damage investors' conﬁdence in the sector. This has a negative
        impact on all companies in the sector, not just water companies, impacting on
        their ability to access investment and the cost of that investment.

Any damage caused to the environment

6.23. We are concerned that South East Water's poor asset management may have
      added unnecessary stress or harm to the environment. There are some aspects
      of South East Water's failings, such as those that resulted in excessive leakage
      (Section 3.3.2), which could have led, and continue to lead to more water
      abstraction than necessary, putting greater pressure on water levels and the
      environment more generally. There is also a risk to water quality from its
      insufﬁcient inspection and maintenance of service reservoirs.

Whether the contraventions or failures were or are of a trivial nature

6.24.   We have outlined above the serious ongoing nature and duration of these
        contraventions and the impact on customers. We therefore do not consider
        these contraventions to be trivial.

Whether the contravention or possibility of contravention would have been apparent
to a diligent company

6.25. As we set out in Section 3 and Section 4, South East Water's contraventions
      results from the failings in its maintenance, planning and operational response,
      which we consider should have been apparent to a diligent water company.


6.26. South East Water had identiﬁed, at Board-level, known vulnerabilities and risks
        to its supply system resilience. It also had information available to it that showed
        it had problems with asset maintenance and that this was negatively impacting
        its ability to comply with its regulatory obligations. These issues should have

                                              185
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



        been addressed, primarily through asset maintenance and appropriate
        planning, including thorough root cause analysis. Those risks that were unable
        to be resolved during the Relevant Period should have been mitigated by an
        appropriate operational response to supply interruptions.


6.27.   In 2018, Ofwat found Thames Water in breach of section 37 WIA91 and the
        equivalent at the time of Licence Condition P. 606 These breaches were largely
        due to how Thames Water designed and implemented its contractual
        arrangements for reducing leakage, which meant that the company had
        insufﬁcient oversight and control of its leakage performance. We have found
        poor planning with respect to South East Water's leakage and supply
        interruption PCLs and a combined failure to maintain key assets, which
        exacerbated known vulnerabilities within its Eastern region and meant that its
        supply system was less resilient during the Relevant Period. The 2018 Thames
        Water case, in which absent commitments we would have imposed a 3% (£25.3
        million) penalty, should have put South East Water's Board on notice that
        compliance with section 37 WIA91 is important.

6.28. As set out in paragraph 3.6, on 19 June 2018, Ofwat conducted an industry-wide
      review into the 2018 Freeze Thaw Event with the expectation for "every water
      company to take action on the back of this review and the industry as a whole to
      transform how it works together in situations like this." 607 As such, we consider
      that South East Water should have been put on notice about the importance of
      supply system resilience and its operational response to supply interruption
      events. We consider it should have implemented the lessons from the 2018
      Freeze Thaw Event before the Events within the Relevant Period. In its review,
        Ofwat clearly stated that, "We will take action if [companies] don’t rise to this
        challenge." 608


6.29. In 2019, Ofwat imposed a penalty and accepted commitments equivalent to a
        6.7% penalty on Southern Water for a breach of its principal duty under section
        94 WIA91 and of Licence Condition P. 609 While this decision concerned deliberate
        misreporting, it also highlighted a failure to adequately maintain and operate its
        wastewater assets. At the time, we wrote to the CEOs of all companies 610 to

    Ofwat, Notice of imposition of a financial penalty on Thames Water Utilities Limited, August 2018, link.

    PN 26/18: Hard lessons for water sector following “Beast from the East” review - Ofwat

    PN 26/18: Hard lessons for water sector following “Beast from the East” review - Ofwat.

    Ofwat, letter to water companies, 28 July 2019, link.

        ensure they were aware of the ﬁnding and asked them to consider whether they
        were conﬁdent that the events which occurred at Southern Water could not
        happen in their own organisations, emphasising that companies' Boards should
        ensure that the governance and assurance processes provide them with the
        right information about company performance. We used our letter as an
        opportunity to reinforce our message to the sector that we will not tolerate
        companies that "show scant regards to their responsibilities to customers and
        the environment" and noted that "companies should expect us to step up the
        sanctions we impose for breaches of this nature." This has been borne out in our
        recent decision making in relation to our industrywide investigation into
        breaches of section 94 WIA91 in the wastewater sector. Section 94 is the
        wastewater equivalent of the section 37 WIA91 principal duty. South East Water
        should therefore be on notice of the potential consequences of failing to comply
        with this fundamental duty.

Precedents set under equivalent provisions for other utilities

6.30. We are not aware of any relevant precedent decisions by other regulators in
      respect of provisions equivalent to section 37 WIA91 and Licence Condition P12.

Conclusion on the appropriateness of ﬁnancial penalty and its broad level

6.31.   Having considered the factors above, we are satisﬁed that a ﬁnancial penalty is
        appropriate in this case. As discussed in Section 4 above, we have found that
        South East Water contravened its duties and obligations under section 37 WIA91
        and Licence Condition P12 and that these contraventions were serious in nature.
        The contraventions are interrelated to an extent and their combined effect
        impacts on the seriousness of South East Water's conduct as a whole. Therefore,
        we propose to impose a single penalty, rather than separate penalties in respect
        of each breach.

6.32. In setting the broad penalty level, we have taken the factors above into account,
        as well as both the cumulative effect of all breaches and the degree of overlap
        between them. We recognise that the scope of this investigation and the impact
        of South East Water's failings we identiﬁed in this document concern the
        Eastern region, not the entire area for which South East Water is the undertaker.
        We considered whether this should be reﬂected in the level of penalty we
        propose to impose on the company. The Eastern region covers the majority of
        the company's WRZs and, as discussed above and shown in our ﬁndings, the
        failings with respect to this region and the impact on customers (discussed
        further below) were widespread, longstanding and particularly serious. We have
        previously warned companies that penalties for future breaches would increase
        and that we would take action if the industry did not improve its operational

                                            187
      Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                               on South East Water Limited



         response to incidents. We are satisﬁed that these factors and considerations
         justify setting the proposed broad level of penalty at a relatively high level of 8%
         of the company's relevant turnover.


6.33. We also consider that this broad penalty level is consistent with our approach in
      previous decisions dealing with compliance with core duties. As above,
         companies have been on notice since our case against Thames Water in 2018,
         which resulted in ﬁnancial commitments equivalent to a ﬁne of 3% of turnover
         of their duty to comply with section 37 WIA91. Ofwat made clear in its open letter
         to industry at the time it reached settlement decision with Southern Water
         equivalent to a ﬁne of 6.7% that going forward companies could expect ﬁnes to
         increase for serious breaches of principal duties.


6.34. We have since issued a ﬁne of 9% of turnover to Thames Water for serious
      breaches of section 94 WIA91, the wastewater equivalent to section 37 WIA91,
      and accepted undertakings and redress packages calibrated according to the
      severity of the breach. We consider that the proposed ﬁne is consistent with this
      practice given the severity and duration of the failures we have uncovered in
      SEW’s Eastern Region and wider governance.

Aggravating and mitigating factors
6.35. As set out in our Statement of Policy, other aggravating and mitigating factors
      may be taken into consideration to make further adjustments to the penalty
      level. We are satisﬁed that certain further adjustments are warranted in South
      East Water's case. We explain this in detail below in relation to the following
      factors we have considered:
     •     Repeated contraventions or failures.
     •     The continuation of a contravention or failure.
     •     Any involvement of senior management.
     •     Any attempts to conceal the contravention or failure.
     •     The level of cooperation with any investigation carried out.
     •     The proactive reporting of the contravention or failure to the enforcement
           authority.
     •     Taking appropriate action to acknowledge and rectify the contravention or
           failure.
     •     Activities to provide restitution and compensation.

Repeated contraventions or failures




                                              188
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



6.36. Ofwat has not previously taken enforcement action against South East Water for
      a breach of section 37 WIA91 or Licence Condition P12. This is not a relevant
        factor in our determination of an appropriate penalty level.

Continuation of a breach

6.37.   Although we have found that the breaches in this case have existed for a
        prolonged period and may still be ongoing, as we have not taken enforcement
        action against South East Water previously for a breach of the relevant
        provisions, there is no continuation of a breach. We therefore make no
        adjustment to the broad level of penalty with respect to this factor.

Any involvement of senior management

6.38. South East Water's senior management and Board failed to ensure that the
      company had proper processes and internal controls in place to ensure they
        understood and were providing appropriate oversight and scrutiny of the
        company's compliance with its obligations, and to provide a robust challenge
        and assurance to the reported levels of performance.


6.39. There is also evidence that its senior management and Board were aware of
      many of the problems identiﬁed but failed to ensure that they were addressed in
      a robust and timely manner. We consider this failure an aggravating factor that
      warrants an uplift to the broad level of the penalty.

Any attempts to conceal the contravention or failure

6.40. We have seen no evidence that the company sought to conceal the
        contravention or failure identiﬁed by our investigation. We therefore do not
        propose to make any adjustments to the penalty level in this respect.

Level of cooperation with the investigation

6.41.   South East Water has provided us with a large amount of information since we
        opened our investigation pursuant to our section 203 WIA91 powers. Being open
        and honest with the regulator is a standard expectation which we apply to all
        companies we investigate.


6.42.   Throughout the investigation we have sought to engage with South East Water
        constructively to address the root cause of its supply interruptions to achieve
        better outcomes for its customers in the Eastern region, including by preparing
        an action plan to address the underlying causes for the supply issues we have
        identiﬁed. While we consider the company should have been more proactive in
        addressing the feedback we provided during the investigation regarding the root
        cause of the incidents and on the action plan we asked it prepares, we do not

                                            189
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



        make any changes to the penalty level with respect to the level of cooperation by
        South East Water during this investigation.

Proactive reporting of the contravention or failure to the enforcement authority

6.43. South East Water has not proactively reported the failures covered by this
        investigation to Ofwat. Instead, as discussed in Section 3, we have opened this
        investigation as a result of the company's repeated poor performance with
        respect to supply interruptions, and its lack of ownership of the issues despite
        engagement with the regulators, including the CEO and Chair. We note that
        South East Water has not, to date, acknowledged that the contraventions and
        failings identiﬁed through our investigation constitute a breach of section 37
        WIA91 or Licence Condition P12.


6.44.   On the contrary, the company has continued to provide assurances to us via its
        annual Ring-Fencing Certiﬁcate and Risk and Compliance Statement about the
        adequacy of its systems, processes and resources to enable it to meet its
        statutory and licence obligations. We therefore do not propose to make any
        mitigating adjustments to the penalty level in this respect.

Taking appropriate action to acknowledge and rectify the contraventions

6.45. As above, South East Water has not, to date, acknowledged that the issues
      identiﬁed in our investigation constitute a breach of section 37 WIA91 or Licence
        Condition P12. We note in the 2018 Thames Water case, a mitigating factor was
        the seriousness with which the company was accepting and approaching
        rectiﬁcation of its leakage reduction performance issues. 611


6.46. We recognise that South East Water has, at our request, prepared an Action Plan
        which has identiﬁed additional steps to address some supply system resilience
        risks. However, given the nature and extent of our concerns in this investigation,
        this Action Plan is not sufﬁcient to rectify the contraventions identiﬁed. Firstly, it
        does not adequately identify, and therefore cannot address, root cause(s) of the
        supply resilience issues. This is recognised by South East Water itself when it
        notes that "these short-term responses are no substitute to the proposals in our
        business plan for 2025 to 2030 addressing the root causes of our supply system
        resilience issues, and which by their nature, scale, the time required for design,
        planning and delivery, must be included in our multi-year investment




611
   Ofwat, Notice of imposition of a financial penalty on Thames Water Utilities Limited, August 2018, p.39,
link.

                                                    190
        Notice of Ofwat’s proposal to issue an enforcement order and impose a penalty
                                 on South East Water Limited



        programme for the next AMP".612 Secondly, it does not address the full breadth of
        our ﬁndings in Section 3 and 4 of this document so it cannot be said to be
        appropriate action to acknowledge and rectify the contraventions. Finally, with
        respect to the ﬁnancial package of investment it involves we query the value of
        the "new" schemes in the Further Updated Action Plan and do not consider it
        represents sufﬁcient redress to the customers.


6.47.   Despite South East Water's claim that its PR24 business plan will address our
        concerns, Ofwat remains concerned that the company has not sufﬁciently
        undertaken a comprehensive root cause analysis to identify the problems and
        ﬁnd the most appropriate solutions as it is required to do as part of maintaining
        an economical and efﬁcient water supply system. As such, South East Water
        fails to recognise the full scale and scope of its failure to comply with section 37
        WIA91 and Licence Condition P12. There remain problems that we have
        identiﬁed in Sections 3 and 4 that it has yet to rectify or commit to rectifying,
        particularly the fundamental issue of its failure to conduct thorough root cause
        analysis so the supply resilience issues in its Eastern region can be properly
        understood. We are also concerned about the lack of ownership of these issues
        by South East Water. We consider this lack of acceptance of responsibility for
        the issues identiﬁed to be an aggravating factor that warrants an uplift to the
        broad level of penalty.

Activities to provide restitution and compensation

6.48. During Ofwat's investigation South East Water has shared ﬁgures in relation to
      the value of payments it made to the household customers affected by the
        Events in the Relevant Period. There was a total of 286,645 household customers
        affected by the Events in the Relevant Period with 52,305 of these customers
        receiving a compensation payment. 613

        affected by each event. These customers can be affected in different ways; for
        example, they may have experienced a supply interruption, low pressure, or
        discoloration of their water. Not all of those matters make a customer eligible for

7. How to make representations or objections

7.1.   Pursuant to sections 20(1)(c) and 22A(4) WIA91, Ofwat, in publishing a notice of a
       proposal to issue an enforcement order under section 18 WIA91 and impose a
       penalty under section 22A(1) WIA91, is required to specify a period, of no less than
       21 days from the date of publication of the notice, within which representations or
       objections with respect to the proposed penalty may be made. Accordingly, any
       such representations or objections should be made so as to be received by Ofwat
       by 13 April 2026.


7.2. Representations should clearly state as to which investigation(s) they relate and
       be sent to the following address:

        mailto: enforcement@ofwat.gov.uk; or
        by post to: Ofwat, Centre City Tower, 7 Hill Street, Birmingham, B5 4UA.


       We may publish copies or summaries of representations and objections we
       receive in response to this notice on our website. Please indicate if you would like
       your response to remain unpublished.

7.3. Information provided in response to this notice, including personal information,
       may be published or disclosed in accordance with access to information
       legislation – primarily the Freedom of Information Act 2000 (FoIA), the Data
       Protection Act 2018, and the Environmental Information Regulations 2004.


7.4. If persons making representations or objections to this notice would like the
     information they provide to be treated as conﬁdential, please be aware that,
       under the FoIA, there is a statutory ‘Code of Practice’ which deals, among other
       things, with obligations of conﬁdence. In view of this, it would be helpful if in
       making representations or obligations it is explained why you regard the
       information provided as conﬁdential. If we receive a request for disclosure of the
       information we will take full account of this explanation, but we cannot give an
       assurance that we can maintain conﬁdentiality in all circumstances. An
       automatic conﬁdentiality disclaimer generated by your IT system will not, of itself,
       be regarded as binding on Ofwat.
